<p>This is a reimagined version of the Tampa City Council agenda. It removes legalese from the descriptions, parses Background details from the Summary Sheet when available, and links to supporting documents. Also included is a zoning map with current applications. Document links point to our mirrored copies for long-term stability. For original documents, refer to the official draft agenda from the clerk in Onbase.</p>

<p class="agenda__source"><i class="agenda__source-icon fa-solid fa-file-lines"></i> <a href="https://tampagov.hylandcloud.com/251agendaonline/Meetings/ViewMeeting?id=2941&amp;doctype=1">City Clerk's Draft Agenda in Onbase</a></p>

<section class="agenda__section">
<h2 id="consent-agenda">Consent Agenda</h2>
<h3 class="agenda__committee" id="public-safety-committee">Public Safety Committee</h3>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28436">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28436">1</a> File No. PS26-28436</h3>
<p>(Part 1 of 2)(See Item 2) Resolution approving the acceptance of grant funds for payment of overtime towards Tampa Violence Reduction and Prevention award date 7/31/2026 for project period through 9/30/2028 in the amount of $178,224 from the United States Department of Justice, Office of Justice Programs, Bureau of Justice Assistance, from the FY 2025 Edward Bryne Memorial Justice Assistance Grant (JAG) program - Local Formula.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28436">
<h4 id="financial-28436" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution approves the acceptance of the grant award not to exceed $178,224 from United States Department of Justice for the Edward Byrne Memorial Justice Assistance Grant Program - Local Formula for police overtime. Funds will be appropriated for the Tampa Police Department within the Police grants Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted (FY25)</dt><dd>$178,224</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$178,224</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Police Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Police Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">DOJ-Justice Assistance Grant</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY25 · 12500.228990.331211.1003044</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue Increase</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa applied for grant funding through the United States Department of Justice, Office of Justice Programs, Bureau of Justice Assistance from the Edward Byrne Memorial Justice Assistance Grant Program – Local Formula.  The Office of Justice Programs has approved the City of Tampa’s application for funding and awarded $178,224 under the FY 2025 Edward Byrne Memorial Justice Assistance Grant Program - Local Formula and the City of Tampa desires to accept the awarded grant number 15PBJA-25-GG-03469-JAGX.  The grant funds will be used for payment of overtime hours spent by Tampa Police Department officers for crime reduction initiatives to reduce community violence through a targeted, data-driven overtime initiative designed to reduce violent crime and improve public safety.</p>
<p>Entering into this agreement is determined to be in the best interest of the City of Tampa.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28445">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28445">3</a> File No. PS26-28445</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the annual submission of the Federal Equitable Sharing Agreement and Certification Report for FY end date 9/30/2026 relating to participation in the Federal Equitable Sharing Program.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa Police Department participates in numerous task forces and joint investigations with various federal law enforcement agencies.  Property and cash used to facilitate the crimes investigated jointly by said agencies are subject to forfeiture under federal law and equitably distributed among the agencies who participate in the Equitable Sharing Program with the Department of Justice.  The Federal Equitable Sharing Agreement must be submitted annually and requires a certification of the prior fiscal year funds and compliance with federal regulations.  Execution of the attached Federal Equitable Sharing Agreement and Certification is a condition of the City of Tampa’s continued participation in equitable sharing of seized property and cash.  Shared assets must be used for law enforcement purposes such as training and drug, gang and prevention awareness programs.  Entering into this agreement is determined to be in the best interest of the City of Tampa.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>

</ul>
</div>
</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28520">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28520">4</a> File No. PS26-28520</h3>
<p>(Part 1 of 2)(See Item 5) Resolution of the City of Tampa approving Modification No. 2 to the Federally Funded Subgrant Agreement (No. H0957) between the City of Tampa and State of Florida Division of Emergency Management for the City of Tampa, Fire Station #16, Wind Retrofit (Project No. 4486-048-R); authorizing execution thereof by the Mayor of the City of Tampa and attestation by the City Clerk; providing an effective date of October 31, 2026, nunc pro tunc.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28520">
<h4 id="financial-28520" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution authorizes Modification No. 2 to the federally funded subgrant agreement between the City of Tampa and the State of Florida Division of Emergency Management, extending the agreement through October 31, 2027, and increasing the federal award by $159,952.50, consisting of $152,335.80 in additional federal funding and $7,616.70 in federal contingency funding. The modification also requires an additional City match of $17,772.50, consisting of $16,926.20 in matching funds and $846.30 in matching contingency funds. The revised total project amount for the FS No. 16 – Wind Mitigation &amp; Generator Project is $726,743.00, consisting of $622,923.30 in federal funds and $31,145.40 in federal contingency funding within the General Government Grants Capital Projects Fund, and $69,213.70 in City matching funds and $3,460.60 in City matching contingency funds within the Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$177,725</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted</dt><dd>$159,953</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$159,952.50</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Government Grants Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Facilities Building/Facility Management Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Federal Grant - Other</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">FS No 16 Wind Mitigation &amp; Generator</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30416.210700.331900.1002522</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$152,335.80</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Government Grants Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Facilities Building/Facility Management Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">FS No 16 Wind Mitigation &amp; Generator</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">31400.210700.563000.1002522</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$846.30</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects - Pay Go</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Facilities Building/Facility Management Capital Project Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Capital Outlay Budget Reserve</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">FS No 16 Wind Mitigation &amp; Generator</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">31400.210700.569999.1002522</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>Modification No. 2 reinstates the Federally Funded Subgrant Agreement No. H0957and extends the period of performance to October 31, 2027, andincreases the Federal Funding by $152,335.80 for the maximum amount payable under the Agreement to $622,923.30.</p>
<p>It also updates the Budget and Scope of Work to reallocate funds between budget categories to align with contractor billing, provide timeline updates, and update Sub-Recipient Management Cost language. The budget has been adjusted to reallocate costs among materials, labor, and fees.</p>
<p>The total initial agreement amount has been updated to $692,137.00 with a Federal share of $622,923.30 and a match share of $69,213.70. In addition, this project has an estimated $34,606.00 in contingency funds ($31,145.40 federal share and $3,460.60 non-federal).</p>
<p>Contingency funds are not automatically available for use. Prior to their release, contingency funds must be re-budgeted to another direct cost category and identified. Post-award changes to the budget require prior written approval from the Division (FDEM). The written request should demonstrate what unforeseen conditions related to the project arose that required the use of contingency funds.</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>In April 2023, pursuant to Resolution 2023-378, the City Council of the City of Tampa approved Federally Funded Subgrant Agreement No. H0957 between the City and the Florida Department of Emergency Management for the City of Tampa Fire Station #16 Wind Retrofit project, in an amount not to exceed $494,116.20, with a required City match of $54,901.80.</p>
<p>In June 2025, pursuant to Resolution 2025-527, the City Council approved Modification No. 1, which extended the period of performance to October 31, 2026.</p>
<p>Modification No. 2 to the Agreement reinstates and extends the period of performance through October 31, 2027, increases federal funding by $152,335.80 and federal contingency funding by $7,616.70, and increases the City match requirement by $16,926.20 and City matching contingency by $846.30.</p>
<p>These modifications bring the revised total project amount for the FS No. 16 – Wind Mitigation &amp; Generator Project to $726,743.00, consisting of $622,923.30 in federal funds and $31,145.40 in federal contingency funding within the General Government Grants Capital Projects Fund, and $69,213.70 in City matching funds and $3,460.60 in City matching contingency funds within the Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects Fund.</p>
</details>

<h3 class="agenda__committee" id="neighborhood-and-community-affairs-committee">Neighborhood and Community Affairs Committee</h3>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28242">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28242">6</a> File No. NCA26-28242</h3>
<p>Resolution making certain changes in the budget of the City of Tampa for the fiscal year ending September 30, 2027; approving the transfer, reallocation and/or appropriation of $600,000 from the Citywide Dock and Boardwalk Replacement project to the Picnic Island Pier Restoration project within the CIT Program 5 Capital Projects Fund and the CIT Program 6 Capital Projects Fund.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>

<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure-decrease "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$444,271.93</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CIT Program 5 FY17 - FY21 Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Citywide Dock &amp; Boardwalk Repl</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30210.225800.563000.1001426</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Decrease</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure-decrease "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$155,728.07</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CIT Program 6 FY22 - FY26 Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Citywide Dock &amp; Boardwalk Repl</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30211.225800.563000.1001426</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Decrease</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$40,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CIT Program 5 FY17 - FY21 Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Professional Services Capital</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Picnic Island Pier Restoration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30210.225800.560005.1003040</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$404,271.93</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CIT Program 5 FY17 - FY21 Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Picnic Island Pier Restoration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30210.225800.563000.1003040</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$155,728.07</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CIT Program 6 FY22 - FY26 Capital Projects</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Picnic Island Pier Restoration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">30211.225800.563000.1003040</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>On January 23, 2026, a 20-foot section of the Picnic Island Pier collapsed into the water, resulting in the closure of the pier. Risk Management notified the insurance company, which subsequently engaged an engineer to assess the damage. In April 2026, an adjacent section of the pier also collapsed. The engineering report recommended replacing both collapsed sections, as well as a third section, to ensure the pier’s safety and restore its functionality for public use.</p>
<p>This financial resolution reallocates a total of $600,000 from the Citywide Dock and Boardwalk Replacement project to the Picnic Island Pier Restoration project, consisting of $444,271.93 from the CIT Program 5 Capital Projects Fund and $155,728.07 CIT Program 5 Capital Projects Fund.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28310">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28310">7</a> File No. NCA26-28310</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City of Tampa, Florida accepting a donation of youth uniforms from Circle K and National Alliance for Youth Sports (NAYS) that is valued at thirty-two thousand five hundred dollars ($32,500) to support the City of Tampa Parks &amp; Recreation Department, Athletic Division; authorizing the Mayor of the City of Tampa to Execute the acceptance of said donation; providing an effective date Nunc Pro Tunc to October 13, 2026.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa desires to accept a donation of a youth uniforms from Circle K and National Alliance for Youth Sports (NAYS) (“Donor”) at an estimated value of Thirty-Two Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($32,500).  Acceptance of the donation is being undertaken in a manner consistent with the City Acceptance of Gifts &amp; Donation Policy.  Donor has agreed to execute the Instrument of Donation confirming its intention to donate youth uniforms valued at Thirty-Two Thousand Five Hundred Dollars ($32,500) to support the City of Tampa Parks and Recreation Department (a copy of which is attached hereto as Exhibits A and B).  City Council does hereby affirm that it would be beneficial to the residents of the City to approve acceptance of the donation.</p>
</details>

</ul>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28433">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28433">8</a> File No. NCA26-28433</h3>
<p>Resolution of the City of Tampa, Florida, approving Amendment No. 1 to the Grant Agreement (RES15) between the City of Tampa and the Florida Department of Environmental Protection for the Picnic Island Nature-Based Solutions Project.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The grant agreement is to design nature-based solutions (NBS) at Picnic Island Park.</p>
<p>Amendment No. 1 deletes Attachment 1 in its entirety and replaces it with Attachment 1-A; deletes Attachment 2 in its entirety and replaces it with Attachment 2-A; deletes Attachment 3 in its entirety and replaces it with Attachment 3-A; deletes Attachment 5 in its entirety and replaces it with Attachment 5-A; and deletes Exhibit A in its entirety and replaces it with Exhibit A-1. The total funding amount of $2,000,000 is unchanged from the original agreement. There is no required match for this project.</p>
<p>The updates to Attachment 1-A include general formatting and updates to Section 23.</p>
<p>Subsection b. regarding updated references to specific civil rights laws and regulations.</p>
<p>The updates to Attachment 2-A include general formatting.</p>
<p>The updates made to Attachment 3-A include general formatting updates; adding Habitat Survey results to the Task Description for Task 2; adding not-to-exceed cost amounts for Tasks 2-5; breaking down the previous Task 4 into Task 4.1 and 4.2 to allow for more frequent reimbursement; updating the project timeline to add dates for Task 4.1 and 4.2; and updating the budget detail by task to align the budget with the project contract.</p>
<p>The updates to Attachment 5-A include general formatting. The updates made to Exhibit A-1 include updating Task 4 in the quarterly reporting template to include 50% for Intermediate design and adding a final project monitoring plan deliverable that had previously been left out.</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>As stated in resolution 2026-658, passed on August 27,2026, the City approved an award for the Delivery and Installation of Trees for the Tree-Mendous Tampa Program (SMS) to Nelson’s Tree Farm and Nursery, Inc. for use by the Parks and Recreation Department. The term of the award is for a (1) one-year term with three (3) additional (1) one-year renewal options.</p>
<p>This financial resolution appropriates $50,000 for the Central Tampa Tree Trust Fund, Westshore Tampa Tree Trust Fund, University Tampa Tree Trust Fund, New Tampa Tree Trust Fund, and the South Tampa Tree Trust Fund.</p>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28527">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28527">15</a> File No. NCA26-28527</h3>
<p>Resolution making certain changes in the budget of the City of Tampa for the fiscal year ending September 30, 2027; approving the transfer, reallocation and/or appropriation of $125,000 from the Belmont Heights Athletic Field Improvements project to the Jackson Heights Athletic Field Improvements project within the Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects Fund.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28527">
<h4 id="financial-28527" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This financial resolution reallocates $125,000 from the Belmont Heights Athletic Field Improvements project to the Jackson Heights Athletic Field Improvements project within the Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$125,000</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Previously authorized spending this resolution removes from the budget."><dt>Spending reduced</dt><dd>$125,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure-decrease "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$125,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects - Pay Go</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Belmont Heights Athletic Field Improvements</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">31400.225800.563000.1002961</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$125,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects - Pay Go</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Parks Capital Project Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Infrastructure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Jackson Heights Athletic Field Improvements</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">31400.225800.563000.1002962</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>This financial resolution reallocates $125,000 from the Belmont Heights Athletic Field Improvements project to the Jackson Heights Athletic Field Improvements project within the Utilities Services Tax Capital Projects Fund.</p>
<p>In May 2026, pursuant to Financial Resolution 2026-350, the City Council of the City of Tampa approved the transfer of $207,500 to the Belmont Heights Athletic Field Improvements project. The project improvements will be completed under budget, leaving $125,000 in unneeded funds for this project. Accordingly, this request seeks approval to transfer the remaining $125,000 to the Jackson Heights Athletic Field Improvements project.</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>

<p>Resolution authorizing Change Order 1 Final to contract 23-C-00013 Site 2 Morris Bridge Filter Building Improvements - Rebid, between the City of Tampa and Trias Construction LLC, a decrease of $78,141.38 and final payment in the amount of $10,000.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28196">
<h4 id="financial-28196" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This Resolution authorizes Change Order No. 1 Final, decreases the existing contract with Trias Construction LLC for Site 2 Morris Bridge Filter Building Improvements within the Morris Bridge Campus-Continuity of Operations (COOP) Center Project by $78,141.38, and provides final payment of $10,000 from the Water Renewal &amp; Replacement Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$10,000</dd></div><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Previously authorized spending this resolution removes from the budget."><dt>Spending reduced</dt><dd>$78,141</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure-decrease "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$78,141.38</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Water - Renewal &amp; Replacement</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Water Capital Costs Production</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Improv Other Than Bldgs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Morris Bridge Campus COOP Ctr</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">40700.244500.563001.1002181</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Decrease</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$10,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Water - Renewal &amp; Replacement</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Water Capital Costs Production</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Retainage Payable</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Morris Bridge Campus COOP Ctr</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">40700.244500.205001.1002181</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<ol><li>Contract Title: 23-C-00013 Site 02 – Morris Bridge Filter Building Improvements a. Address of Project: Citywide b. Number of bidders/submitters: N/A c. Names of bidders/submitters: N/A d. Dollar amount: Decrease ($78,141.38) e. Why the awardee was selected: Contract was competitively bid.</li><li>Contractor/Firm: Trias Construction</li><li>Business Case: This project provided for improvements to the existing Filter Building, associated site improvements, and existing facility demolition at the City’s Morris Bridge site.</li><li>Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Statement: Trias Construction LLC, the prime contractor, has utilized one (1) City of Tampa-certified firm for subcontracting.</li><li>Sustainability and Resiliency: N/A</li><li>Community Engagement: The Contract Administration Department’s Engineering and Inspection team will assist the Contractor in all communication efforts to ensure transparency and integration of the stakeholders.</li><li>Project: a. Contract delivery method: This was a Construction project.</li></ol><p>Work included retrofitting the roof of the Filter Building, removing sediment and vegetation at the three stormwater ponds, and demolishing tanks, building, concrete pavement, and piping.</p>
<p>This Changer Order 1 Final provides for credit to the City for unused contingency and release of retainage.</p>
<p>b. Placemaking: N/A c. Time, Quality Assurance, Cost Monitoring (Budget) • 0 consecutive calendar days.</p>
<p>• The e-Builder project management software system will be utilized to optimize the monitoring of schedules and costs. This software was recently purchased by the City of Tampa and is a premier system for construction project management.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28274">

<p>(Part 1 of 2)(See Item 18) Resolution accepting the proposal of Granger Maintenance &amp; Construction, Inc. pertaining to Contract 26-C-00003 FY26 Miscellaneous PS Riser Replacements, in the amount of $798,000.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28274">
<h4 id="financial-28274" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This Resolution provides funding in the amount of $798,000 for the Miscellaneous Pumping Station Riser Replacements Project for use by the Wastewater Department within the Wastewater Future Debt Capital Projects Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$798,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$798,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Wastewater Future Debt Capital Projects Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Wastewater Capital Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Improv Other Than Bldgs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Miscellaneous Pumping Station Riser Replacements Project</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41154.243200.563001.1002905</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<ol><li>Contract Title: 26-C-00003 FY26 Miscellaneous PS Riser Replacements a. Address of Project: Various Locations b. Number of bidders/submitters: 2 (two) c.    Names of bidders/submitters: • Granger Maintenance &amp; Construction, Inc.</li><li>Contractor/Firm: Granger Maintenance &amp; Construction, Inc.</li><li>Business Case: This project provides the furnish all labor, materials, and equipment for repair of 4 pumping stations including removal and replacement of existing pump bases, discharge piping, valves, and fittings, installing line-stop, bypass tee, and valves essential for bypassing the stations with all associated work required for a complete project in accordance with the Contract Documents.</li><li>Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Statement: Granger Maintenance &amp; Construction, Inc, the prime contractor, is utilizing one (1) City of Tampa-certified firm for subcontracting.</li><li>Sustainability and Resiliency: Rehabilitation of the infrastructure will restore the structural integrity, provide emergency bypassing connection, and improve pumping capacity.</li><li>Community Engagement: The Wastewater Public Relations and CAD Construction, Engineering and Inspection (CAD-CEI) team will assist the Contractor in the communication efforts to ensure transparency and integration of the stakeholders.</li><li>Project: a. Contract delivery method: This is a construction project.</li></ol><p>Background:</p>
<p>• KAT Construction and Materials Inc.</p>
<p>d. Dollar amount: $798,000.00 e. Why the awardee was selected: The lowest responsible responsive bidder was selected pursuant to FS 255.20.</p>
<p>The rehabilitation will extend the useful life of the pumping stations and prevent wastewater overflow at the stations.</p>
<p>b. Placemaking: N/A c. Time, Quality Assurance, Cost Monitoring (Budget) • 270 consecutive calendar days.</p>
<p>• A full-time City of Tampa Construction Inspector will be on-site during the project to ensure quality of construction.</p>
<p>• The e-Builder (Trimble Unity Construct) project management software system will be utilized to optimize the monitoring of schedules and costs. Trimble Unity Construct is a premier system for construction project management.</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<ol><li>Contract Title: 26-C-00046 Ybor City CRA 7th Avenue Street Resurfacing (Phase 3) a. Address of Project: Ybor City 7th Avenue, Tampa, FL 33605.</li><li>Contractor/Firm: Cathcart Construction Company – Florida, LLC</li><li>Business Case: This project provides for constructing pedestrian mobility and intersection improvements along 7th Avenue at the intersections of 17th Street, Angel Oliva Senior Street, and 19th Street. Work shall include clearing and grubbing, selective demolition of existing concrete improvements, tree removal, milling of existing asphalt pavement, installation of optional base, reconstruction of curb ramps at each quadrant of the intersections to achieve ADA compliance, construction of new concrete sidewalk, curb and gutter, installation of detectable warning surfaces, adjustment of existing manholes, valve boxes, and meter boxes, restoration of historic architectural brick roadway and sidewalk pavers within the project limits, installation and relocation of regulatory signing, pavement markings and raised pavement markers, relocation of existing benches, installation of new trash receptacles, and all associated restoration and incidental work necessary to complete the improvements in accordance with the Contract Documents.</li><li>Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Statement: Cathcart Construction Company – Florida, LLC., the prime contractor, is committed to utilizing one (1) city-certified firm for subcontracting.</li><li>Sustainability and Resiliency: Consistent with Redevelopment Action goals within the Ybor City Community Redevelopment Area (CRA) Plan specific to implementation measures meant to define the historic character of Ybor City.</li><li>Community Engagement: The Mobility Public Relations and CAD Construction, Engineering and Inspection (CAD-CEI) team will assist the Contractor in the communication efforts to ensure transparency and integration of the stakeholders.</li><li>Project: a.    Contract delivery method: This is a Design-Bid-Build project.</li></ol><p>Background:</p>
<p>b. Number of bidders/submitters: 3 (three) c.    Names of bidders/submitters: • Cathcart Construction Company – Florida, LLC • Harris-McBurney Company • Ajax Paving Industries of Florida, LLC d. Dollar amount: $928,250.98 e.    Why the awardee was selected: The lowest qualified and responsive bidder was selected pursuant to FS 255.20.</p>
<p>b. Placemaking: N/A c.    Time, Quality Assurance, Cost Monitoring (Budget): • 90 consecutive calendar days.</p>
<p>• A full-time City of Tampa Construction Inspector will be on-site during the project to ensure quality  of construction.</p>
<p>• The Trimble Unity Construct project management software system will be utilized to optimize the monitoring of schedules and costs. Trimble Unity Construct is a premier system for construction project management.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28420">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28420">20</a> File No. INF26-28420</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the renewal of an award between the City of Tampa and Big Dog Express of South Florida, Inc., with a 3.4% Consumer Price Index (CPI) price increase, for the furnishing of Solid Waste Material Hauling Services at McKay Bay Transfer Station (Bid Discount) in an estimated amount of $1,300,000 for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28420">
<h4 id="financial-28420" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution renews the award between the City and Big Dog Express of South Florida, Inc. for the furnishing of Solid Waste Material Hauling Services at McKay Bay Transfer Station (Bid Discount) and provides an estimated amount of $1,300,000, over a 12-month term, subject to annual appropriation, for use by the Department of Solid Waste and Environmental Program Management within the Solid Waste Operations Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$758,100</dd></div></dl>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__future">Plus <strong>$541,900</strong> in future-year amounts subject to annual Council approval.</p>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$758,100</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Transfer Station</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 41400.241500.534007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Estimated Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure is-future"><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$541,900</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Transfer Station</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY28 · 41400.241500.534007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Estimated Expenditure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__subject" title="Council is not committing this fiscal year today; subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>It is recommended that the City renew its existing award with Big Dog Express of South FL, Inc. for the provision of Solid Waste Material Hauling Services at McKay Bay Transfer Station (Bid Discount) for use by the Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management Department for an additional one-year term. The existing award was made March 06, 2025.</p>
<p>This award is in its first renewal term.</p>
<p>Big Dog Express of South Fl, Inc., provides excellent service, quality of product and performance. The Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management Department has reviewed the prices and feels that it is in the best interest of the City to renew the award for an additional one-year term.  Big Dog Express of South FL, Inc., has agreed to renew Solid Waste Material Hauling Services at McKay Bay Transfer Station (Bid Discount) with a Consumer Price Index (CPI) price escalation of 3.4% which is allowed per the bid.</p>
<p>SLBE participation goals were not prescribed in the solicitation because the scope did not meet required EBO parameters; however, it did not preclude participation.</p>
</details>

<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28421">21</a> File No. INF26-28421</h3>
<p>Resolution approving, ratifying and confirming the placement of a Purchase Order for the purchase of a NOxOUT Selective Non-Catalytic Reduction (SNCR) Urea Injection System with Baseline Testing and Modeling Services at the McKay Bay Waste to Energy (WTE) Facility in the estimated amount of $2,198,100.00 from Fuel Tech, Inc.; and approving an agreement, inclusive of Proposal Nos. 21-C-019, 21-M-019 and amended terms and conditions between the City of Tampa and Fuel Tech, Inc. for said purchase for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28421">
<h4 id="financial-28421" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution approves the purchase of the NOxOut Selective Non-Catalytic Reduction (SNCR) Urea Injection System and Baseline Testing and Modeling Services for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management and provides $2,198,100 from the McKay Bay WTE Urea Injection System Upgrade Project within the Solid Waste Capital/Construction Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$2,198,100</dd></div></dl>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>It is recommended that the City approve the purchase order on an emergency basis and enter into an agreement with Fuel Tech, Inc. to provide  NOxOut Selective Non-Catalytic Reduction (SNCR)  Urea Injection System with Baseline Testing and Modeling Services for the McKay Bay Waste to Energy Facility for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management.</p>
<p>SLBE participation goals were not prescribed because the project did not meet required EBO parameters.</p>

<p>It is recommended that the City renew its existing award with Williams Landscape Management Co., Inc. for the provision of Grounds Maintenance at Various Water Department Locations (Sheltered Market Solicitation) for use by the Water Department for an additional one-year period.  The existing award became effective September 21, 2023. This award is in its second renewal period.</p>
<p>Williams Landscape Management Co., Inc. provides satisfactory service, quality of product and performance. The Water Department has reviewed the prices and feels that it is in the best interest of the City to renew the award for an additional one-year period. <p>Pursuant to the review by Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Office, it was determined that the EBO Sheltered Market Solicitation provision would apply in the bid specifications to eligible WMBE and SLBE firms. <article class="agenda-item" id="item-28423">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28423">23</a> File No. INF26-28423</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the bid of Rehrig Pacific Company for the furnishing of Various Universal Refuse Carts in the estimated amount of $921,910 over a 13-month term for use by the Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Management Program.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28423">
<h4 id="financial-28423" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This Resolution approves an award between the City and Rehrig Pacific Company for the furnishing of Various Universal Refuse Carts and provides an estimated amount of $921,910, over a 13-month term, subject to annual appropriation, for use by the Solid Waste Department within the Solid Waste Operations Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$732,800</dd></div></dl>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__future">Plus <strong>$189,110</strong> in future-year amounts subject to annual Council approval.</p>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$732,800</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Cart Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Bulk Purchases</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 41400.239600.563005</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Estimated Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure is-future"><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$189,110</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Solid Waste -  Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Solid Waste Cart Management</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Bulk Purchases</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY28 · 41400.239600.563005</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Estimated Expenditure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__subject" title="Council is not committing this fiscal year today; subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>Pursuant to ITB #1010830242 (26-P-00933), General Conditions, Section 4.27 Default/Re-Award and Section 4.28. Cancellation with Cause, the City of Tampa has defaulted the original awardee because of recurring delivery delays at the request of the Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management Department. Exercising the default/re-award clause in section 4.27, the City desires to re-award the Invitation to Bid to the next low bidder, Rehrig Pacific Company.</p>
<p>It is recommended that the City re-award the bid for Various Universal Reuse Carts to Rehrig Pacific Company for use by Solid Waste Department. The term of the award is for a thirteen-month term with (1) one additional (2) two-year renewal option. The Department has reviewed and approved the bid response from Rehrig Pacific Company and comments were supplied via Department recommendation letter.</p>
<p>Pursuant to the review by Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Office, it was determined SLBE participation goals were not prescribed in the solicitation because the scope did not meet required EBO parameters; however it did not preclude participation.</p>
</details>

<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-19/meeting-2941/28423/26-p-00933_various_universal_refuse_containers_reso_updated.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">26-P-00933_VARIOUS_UNIVERSAL_REFUSE_CONTAINERS_RESO_UPDATED.PDF</a></li>

subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The Department of Solid Waste &amp; Environmental Program Management, Waste to Energy Division has standardized Kinergy SDA &amp; Lime Silo Bin Activator assemblies and parts from Kinergy (a DBA of Carrier Vibrating Equipment Inc.).  Standardization to this equipment ensures cost reduction, interoperability because of familiarity among staff, maintenance efficiency and reduced cost of inventory maintenance for parts.  It is recommended that the City purchase Kinergy SDA &amp; Lime Silo Bin Activator assemblies and parts.</p>
<p>There was no EBO Participation as this purchase is being made under a standardization.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28426">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28426">25</a> File No. INF26-28426</h3>
<p>Resolution approving the bid of PSIC-Pro Serv Industrial Contractors, LLC for the furnishing of Miscellaneous Mechanical Maintenance Services for AWTP (Bid Discount) (Re-Bid) in the estimated amount of $892,629.95 for use by the Wastewater Department; authorizing the Director of Purchasing to purchase said property, supplies, materials or services; providing an immediate effective date.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28426">
<h4 id="financial-28426" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This Resolution approves an award between the City and PSIC-Pro Serv Industrial Contractors, LLC for the furnishing of Miscellaneous Mechanical Maintenance Services for AWTP (Bid Discount) (Re-Bid) and provides an estimated amount of $892,629.95, subject to annual appropriation, for use by the Wastewater Department.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$892,630</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$818,244.12</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Wastewater - Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Wastewater Plant operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Repair and Maintenance Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 41000.243900.546000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Estimated Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$74,385.83</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Wastewater - Operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Wastewater Plant operations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Repair and Maintenance Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY28 · 41000.243900.546000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Estimated Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<ol><li>Department or Location of Project: Wastewater</li><li>Total number of vendors that accessed and downloaded bid: 41</li><li>Number and List of bidders: Two, PSIC-Pro Serv Industrial Contractors, LLC and AFFORDABLE DEVELOPMENT</li><li>Award amount: $892,629.95</li><li>Why the winning bid was selected: The lowest, responsive, and responsible bidder. The City of Tampa Wastewater Department recommends awarding this to PSIC-Pro Serv Industrial Contractors, LLC as the lowest responsive and responsible bidder for Miscellaneous Mechanical Maintenance Services for AWTP (Bid Discount) (Re-Bid).</li></ol><p>It is recommended that the City award Miscellaneous Mechanical Maintenance Services for AWTP (Bid Discount) (Re-Bid) to PSIC-Pro Serv Industrial Contractors, LLC for use by Wastewater.  The term of the award is for a (1) one-year term with (4) four additional (1) one-year) renewal options. The Department has reviewed and approved the bid response from PSIC-Pro Serv Industrial Contractors, LLC and comments were supplied via Department recommendation letter.</p>
<p>Pursuant to the review by Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Office, it was determined that the EBO Bid Discount provision would apply in the bid specifications to eligible SLBE firms.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28450">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28450">26</a> File No. INF26-28450</h3>
<p>Resolution authorizing an Amendment to the Agreement for Consultant Services, in the amount of $164,895, between the City of Tampa and Tetra Tech, Inc. regarding Contract 25-D-00014-CSA01 Coachman Avenue Wastewater Improvements Design.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28450">
<h4 id="financial-28450" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This Resolution provides funding in the amount of $164,895 for the Coachman Avenue Wastewater Improvements Project for use by the Wastewater Department within the Wastewater Capital Construction Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$164,895</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$164,895.00</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">Wastewater - Capital/Construction</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Wastewater Capital Costs</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Professional Services Capital</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">Coachman Avenue Wastewater Improvements</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">41100.243200.560005.1002780</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<ol><li>Contract Title: 25-D-00014 Coachman Avenue Wastewater Improvements Design a. Address of Project: Coachman Avenue, Tampa, FL 33611 b. Names of bidders/submitters: Tetra Tech, Inc.</li><li>Contractor/Firm: Tetra Tech, Inc.</li><li>Equal Business Opportunity (EBO) Statement: Tetra Tech, Inc., the prime contractor, is committed to utilizing no city-certified firms for subcontracting.</li><li>Community Engagement: N/A</li><li>Project: a. Contract delivery method: This is a Design project.</li></ol><p>c. Dollar amount: • The Initial Agreement Amount was $763,691.00, •This Amendment 1 is an additional $164,895.00 which will bring the Total Design Cost to $928,586.00.</p>
<p>3.Business Case: a. Phase 1 of the project provided professional services to develop and evaluate improvement alternatives to prevent future storm-related wastewater overflows along W.</p>
<p>Coachman and W. Alline Avenues.</p>
<p>b. This Phase 2 will provide professional services to further evaluate alternatives and prioritize pump station site selection, develop preliminary pump and wet-well sizing, and prepare an alternatives analysis with recommendations and refined planning-level costs.</p>
<p>5.Sustainability and Resiliency: This project provides professional services to develop and design wastewater system improvements to prevent future storm-related wastewater overflows along W. Coachman and W. Alline Avenues.</p>
<p>b. Placemaking: N/A c. Time, Quality Assurance, Cost Monitoring (Budget) • 210 consecutive calendar days.</p>
<p>• The Trimble Unity Construct project management software system will be utilized to optimize the monitoring of schedules and costs. Trimble Unity Construct is a premier system for construction project management.</p>
</details>

<p class="agenda-item-financial__warning agenda-item-financial__warning--imbalance" role="note">Note: The Summary Sheet shows an imbalance of $110,910 between the decrease and increase amounts. <a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-19/meeting-2941/28529/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Review the source PDF.</a></p>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>This financial resolution adjusts the authorized budgets within the following water and wastewater bond funds: Series 2022 – previously approved by Council and bonds have been issued Series 2024 – previously approved by Council and bonds have been issued This financial resolution prepares the above funds for financial closing during FY2027.</p>
<p>Existing bond proceeds will be spent down by realigning FY2026 expenses from the Water and Wastewater Future Debt Capital Projects Funds and the Water Renewal and Replacement Fund with the outcome of reducing the amount of borrowed funds needed when the City issues the Series 2027 Water and Wastewater Bonds (formerly Water and Wastewater Future Debt Capital Projects Funds) scheduled during the fall of 2027.</p>
<p>The following summarizes the financial resolution by fund: Wastewater Bonds – Series 2022 Capital Projects Fund *Appropriates $668,412.63 of Interest Earnings and Use of Fund Balance *Decreases various wastewater projects by $5,983,612.93 *Increases the H.F. Curren Master Plan Project and Collection System Rehabilitation Contract Project by $6,652,025.56 Wastewater Bonds – Series 2024 Capital Projects Fund *Decreases various wastewater projects by $10,185,952.36 *Increases the H.F. Current Master Plan Project by $9,305,809.23 *Increases Reserves by $880,143.13 Water Bonds – Series 2022 Capital Projects Fund *Decreases various water projects by $14,717,685.32 *Increases the D.L. Tippin Filter Improvements Project and the Citywide Water Main Replacements Project by $14,628,595.53 *Increases Reserves by $89,090.06 Water Bonds – Series 2024 Capital Projects Fund *Decreases various water projects by $15,915,977.95 *Increases the D.L. Tippin Filter Improvements Project by $14,925,191.53 *Increases reserves by $990,786.42</p>
</details>

<p>Resolution approving an Agreement for Limited Legal Services between the City of Tampa and Weiss Serota Helfman Cole &amp; Bierman, P.L., in connection with matters related to Bern’s Steak House, Inc. v. City of Tampa, Case No. 26-CA-010883; Bern’s Steak House, Inc. v. City of Tampa, Case No. 26-CA-010882; and Anthony Mark Daniel v. Alan Clendenin, individually and in his official capacity as Chairman of the Tampa City Council, Case No. 8:26-CV-03021-KKM-LSG, and all related litigation matters.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28528">
<h4 id="financial-28528" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">A Resolution approving an agreement for limited legal services between the City and Weiss Serota in an amount not to exceed $200,000 for use by the Non-Department within the General Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized</dt><dd>$200,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$200,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Non Departmental Admin</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Professional Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.220900.531002</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>

<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City is in need of legal services in connection with Bern’s Steak House, Inc. v. City of Tampa, Case No. 26-CA-010883; Bern’s Steak House, Inc. v. City of Tampa, Case No. 26-CA-010882; and Anthony Mark Daniel v. Alan Clendenin, individually and in his official capacity as Chairman of the Tampa City Council, Case No. 8:26-cv-03021-KKM-LSG, The firm Weiss Serota Helfman Cole &amp; Bierman, P.L. is uniquely qualified to provide legal services and serve as outside counsel for these matters.</p>

<h3 class="agenda__committee" id="development-and-economic-opportunity-committee">Development and Economic Opportunity Committee</h3>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-27675">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-27675">30</a> File No. DEO26-27675</h3>
<p>(Part 1 of 2)(See Item 31) Resolution approving an assistance award/amendment to the Fair Housing Assistance Program Award, Instrument Number FF200KK2600034, between the City of Tampa, Florida and U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development to reimburse the City for case processing, administrative costs, and training for dually filed housing discrimination complaints by the Tampa Office of Human Rights for Fiscal Year 2026 and for training provided to City staff through June 30, 2027 in the amount of seventy-two thousand nine hundred dollars ($72,900).</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-27675">
<h4 id="financial-27675" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">The United States Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) has contracted with the City of Tampa for the investigation, conciliation, and other processing of dually filed housing discrimination complaints by the Tampa Office of Human Rights. In consideration thereof, HUD will contribute $72,900 to be used for conducting education and outreach to persons residing in the geographic area served by the Tampa Office of Human Rights. These funds will be deposited and appropriated within the Housing Grants Fund for such purposes.</p>

class="agenda-item-financial__meta">12200.220800.555000.1003036</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>That the Assistance Award/Amendment to the Fair Housing Assistance Program Award, Instrument Number FF200K2600034, between the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) and City of Tampa (City) for case processing, administrative costs, and training funding for dually filed housing discrimination complaints by the Tampa Office of Human Rights for Fiscal Year 2026 and for training provided to City staff through June 30, 2027, (“Award”), (a copy of which is attached hereto), is approved in its entirety or in substantially similar form.</p>
<p>Assistance Award/Amendment to the Fair Housing Assistance Program Award, Instrument Number FF200K2600034, the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) has contracted with the City of Tampa (City) for case processing, administrative costs, and training funding for dually filed housing discrimination complaints by the Tampa Office of Human Rights for Fiscal Year 2026 and for training provided to City staff through June 30, 2027; and pursuant to the Agreement, HUD will reimburse the City in the amount of Seventy-Two Thousand Nine Hundred Dollars ($72,900) for services rendered through the processing of housing discrimination complaints and prospective training; and the Tampa Office of Human Rights has investigated, conciliated and processed several housing discrimination complaints to conclusion; and pursuant to these services provided and verified by HUD, this amendment provides the method for payment of costs incurred by the City; and HUD has agreed to provide funding prospectively for training through June 30, 2027; and City Council does hereby affirm that it would be beneficial to the residents of the City to enter this Amendment between the City and HUD.</p>
</details>

<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28142">
<h3 class="agenda-item__head"><a class="agenda-item__anchor" href="#item-28142">32</a> File No. DEO26-28142</h3>
<p>(Part 1 of 2)(See Item 33) Resolution of the City of Tampa approving an amendment to the grant agreement between the City of Tampa and Resilient Cities Catalyst, Inc.; Related to the Small Business Extreme Weather Preparedness Program; Extending the period of performance through December 31, 2027.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28142">
<h4 id="financial-28142" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution accepts the extension to the period of performance for the Resilient Cities Catalyst, Inc. Small Business Extreme Weather Preparedness Grant Program. Reimbursement from the grant program in the amount of $70,000 is to be deposited to Development and Economic Opportunity within the General Fund.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Outside funds (grants, fees, reimbursements) the City is committing to receive in the current fiscal year."><dt>Grant or revenue accepted</dt><dd>$70,000</dd></div></dl>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$70,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Economic Opportunity Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Contributions and Donations From Private Sources</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.213700.366000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City of Tampa partnered with Resilient Cities Catalyst, Inc. to create the Small Business Extreme Weather Preparedness Program (also known as Tampa Weatherwise Small Business Grant Program).</p>
<p>Pursuant to Resolution No. 2025-1035, adopted by City Council on November 20, 2025, the City entered into a $70,000 grant agreement with Resilient Cities Catalyst, Inc. to provide financial assistance to qualified small businesses for improvements related to extreme weather preparedness and recovery.</p>
<p>The proposed amendment will extend the period of performance through December 31, 2027, and replace the program timeline contained in Exhibit A of the original Grant Agreement.  The revised timeline allows for continued distribution of program funds, compliance monitoring, and evaluation.</p>
</details>
<div class="agenda-item__docs">
<p class="agenda-item__label">Supporting documents</p>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-19/meeting-2941/28142/summary-sheet-cover-sheet.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Summary Sheet- COVER SHEET</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-19/meeting-2941/28142/rccreso.agreement2025-1035.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">RCCRESO.AGREEMENT2025-1035.PDF</a></li>
<li><a href="https://docs.meetings.tampamonitor.com/2026-11-19/meeting-2941/28142/rccreso.agreement2.pdf" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">RCCRESO.AGREEMENT(2).PDF</a></li>
</ul>
</div>
</article>
<article class="agenda-item" id="item-28430">

<p>(Part 2 of 2)(See Item 32) Resolution making certain changes in the budget of the City of Tampa for the fiscal year ending September 30, 2027; approving the transfer, reallocation and/or appropriation of $70,000 within the General Fund for use by the Development and Economic Opportunity for the Resilient Cities Catalyst Inc.- Small Business Extreme Weather Preparedness Program.</p>

<h4 id="financial-28430" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This financial resolution appropriates $70,000 within the General Fund for use the Development and Economic Opportunity for Resilient Cities Catalyst Inc.- Small Business Extreme Weather Preparedness Program.</p>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$70,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Economic Opportunity Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Contributions and Donations From Private Sources</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.213700.366000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$70,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">General Fund</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Economic Opportunity Administration</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Aids to Private Organizations</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">01100.213700.582000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>Pursuant to Resolution No. 2025-1035, adopted by City Council on November 20, 2025, the City entered into a $70,000 grant agreement with Resilient Cities Catalyst, Inc. to provide financial assistance to qualified small businesses for improvements related to extreme weather preparedness and recovery.</p>
<p>This resolution reappropriates the full grant amount of $70,000, to accomodate the grant extension.</p>

<p>Resolution making certain changes in the budget of the City of Tampa for the fiscal year ending September 30, 2026; approving the transfer, reallocation, and/or appropriation of $5,082.09 within the Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) fund to appropriate Program Income for CDBG program activities.</p>
<details class="agenda-item__details">
<summary>Item details</summary>
<section class="agenda-item-financial" aria-labelledby="financial-28187">
<h4 id="financial-28187" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">The Housing and Community Development Department wishes to appropriate Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) program income received by the City in the amount of $5,082.09 for CDBG program expenses within the Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) Fund.</p>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-revenue "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$5,082.09</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CDBG Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development CDBG</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Eco Envir-Centr Rehab-Mortg Income</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">12100.218500.331504</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Revenue Increase</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$5,082.09</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">CDBG Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development CDBG</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Operating Expense Budget Reserve</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">12100.218500.539999</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure Increase</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>The City’s Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) Federal program generates program income from deferred payment loans. <h4 id="financial-28275" class="agenda-item-section__heading">Financial impact</h4>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__statement">This resolution approves an Agreement between the City and AIDS Service Association of Pinellas, Inc., for the provision of services in support of Persons with Acquired Immune Deficiency Syndrome (AIDS) under the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development HOPWA program in an amount of $245,000 per year for 3 years for a total amount not to exceed $735,000. Subrecipient shall be reimbursed for Tenant-Based Rental Assistance in an amount not to exceed $245,000 per year for 3 years for a total amount not to exceed $735,000 from the Housing and Community Development Department’s HOPWA Grant Fund. Funds in the amount of $245,000 are available within the 2027 HOPWA grant fund. FY2028 and FY2029 funding is contingent upon available 2028 and 2029 HOPWA grant funds.</p>
<dl class="agenda-item-financial__totals"><div class="agenda-item-financial__row" title="Total dollars this resolution authorizes the City to spend in the current fiscal year. Inter-fund transfers are excluded to avoid double-counting."><dt>Spending authorized (FY27)</dt><dd>$245,000</dd></div></dl>
<p class="agenda-item-financial__future">Plus <strong>$490,000</strong> in future-year amounts subject to annual Council approval.</p>
<h5 class="agenda-item-financial__sources-heading">Funding sources</h5>
<ul class="agenda-item-financial__sources"><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure "><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$245,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">HOPWA Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development HOPWA</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">HOPWA Contractual 27</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY27 · 12220.218800.534007.1003022</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure is-future"><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$245,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">HOPWA Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development HOPWA</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">HOPWA Contractual 28</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY28 · 12220.218800.534007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__subject" title="Council is not committing this fiscal year today; subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li><li class="agenda-item-financial__source is-expenditure is-future"><span class="agenda-item-financial__amount">$245,000</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__fund">HOPWA Grants</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__dept">Planning Housing and Community Development HOPWA</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__object">Other-Contractual Services</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__project">HOPWA Contractual 29</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__meta">FY29 · 12220.218800.534007</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__marker">Expenditure</span><span class="agenda-item-financial__subject" title="Council is not committing this fiscal year today; subject to future-year appropriation.">subject to annual appropriation</span></li></ul>
</section>
<p class="agenda-item__label">Background</p>
<p>AIDS Service Association of Pinellas, Inc. was awarded a 3-year HOPWA Agreement to provide Tenant-Based Rental Assistance for persons with HIV/AIDS and their affected resident family members in order to create housing stability and reduce the risk of homelessness in Pasco County.</p>
</details>
